# Funding Proposal

## Project Information

- **Project Title:** Resilient Landscapes and Livelihoods Project
  - **Country(ies):** Ethiopia

- **Accredited Entity:** World Bank

- **Date of first submission:** 2018/06/22

- **Date of current submission:** 2020/03/19

- **Version number:** 0.1.007

## Funding Proposal Details

### Project Overview

The Resilient Landscapes and Livelihoods Project aims to enhance the resilience of Ethiopian landscapes and improve the livelihoods of local communities by implementing sustainable agricultural practices, conservation efforts, and climate-smart infrastructure.

### Objectives

1. **Improve Agricultural Productivity:** Enhance crop yields through precision farming techniques and agroforestry systems.
2. **Conserve Biodiversity:** Protect and restore natural habitats to maintain ecological balance and biodiversity.
3. **Climate Change Adaptation:** Implement climate-smart agricultural practices to mitigate the impacts of climate change on food security.
4. **Livelihood Enhancement:** Provide training and resources for sustainable livelihoods, including access to markets and financial services.

### Budget Breakdown

1. **Land Acquisition and Preparation:** $500,000
   - Land acquisition: $200,000
   - Preparation of land for agriculture: $300,000

2. **Agricultural Infrastructure Development:** $700,000
   - Irrigation systems: $400,000
   - Storage facilities: $150,000
   - Processing and marketing infrastructure: $100,000

3. **Conservation and Biodiversity Protection:** $600,000
   - Reforestation and afforestation projects: $200,000
   - Wildlife conservation programs: $250,000
   - Soil conservation measures: $150,000

4. **Community Engagement and Training:** $400,000
   - Farmer training: $300,000
   - Community outreach programs: $100,000
   - Capacity building for local governments: $50,000

5. **Contingency Fund:** $200,000

6. **Total Project Cost:** $3,700,000

**Project Programme Title:** Resilient Landscapes and Livelihoods Project

**Country(ies):** Ethiopia

**Accredited Entity:** World Bank

**Date of first submission:** 2018/06/22

**Date of current submission:** 2020/03/19

**Version number:** 0.1.007

     The RLLP will be implemented through four integrated components: (i) Green infrastructure and resilient livelihoods; 
     management and reporting. Taken together, the activities of creating resilient landscapes and livelihoods for vicinity 
     cone of the project in that it includes the activities directly related to agricultural practices. Component 1 also includes colla- 
     ciation with local communities on sustainable use of biomass for cooking, degradation in Ethiopia. The key interventions proposed 
     makes the link between sustainable land management and 
     bioenergy options for cooking, and improving the efficiency of 
     practices proposed include those promoting the harvesting of resilient land use made through the introduction of sustaina- 
     Component 2 will create institutions and build capacity that will enable the interventions introduced in Component 1 
     to be sustainably implemented even after watersheds grants, especially with the barrier of weak tenure rights. The projects 
     essential to mobilizing to implement the new practices are: (i) Green infrastructure and resilient livelihoods; 
     management and reporting. Taken together, the activities of creating resilient landscapes and livelihoods for vicinity 
     cone of the project in that it includes the activities directly related to agricultural practices. Component 1 also includes collabora- 
     tion with local communities and stakeholders to identify opportunities, resilient livelihoods, and the productive value 
     incentives for communities to maintain restored landscapes; (ii) land tenure; and (iii) climate change adaptation. The key interventions proposed 
     make the link between sustainable land management and 
     bioenergy options for cooking, and improving the efficiency of fuel production and use. The Environment Policy also 
     includes a focus on the development of a national policy to improve land use, diversified 
     crops, and improved water management; (iv) climate change adaptation; and (v) the promotion of low carbon 
     technologies. Component 2 will create institutions and build capacity that will enable the interventions introduced in Component 1 
     specifically with the barrier of weak tenure rights. The priority of securing land to smallholder farmers is 
     essential to mobilizing to implement the new practices.

     The specific technological solutions implemented under sub-component (i) and (ii) in each watershed will be 
     determined using a participatory community-based approach. The approach used is described in the Community 
     Participatory Watershed Management Guidelines (CPWG), which are based on the principles of the United Nations 
     Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). This approach will result in a number of benefits, 
     as well as ensuring that expected results are achieved. 
     During the participatory planning communities first present 
     their needs and objectives to the Community Participatory Watershed Development Guidelines (CPWG) 
     worda responsibility office (office of Agriculture). The proposi- 
     lation will be based on the following sub-components: (i) Climate 
     change adaptation measures; (ii) Livelihood diversification and 
     sustainable livelihoods; (iii) Land restoration and watershed management; (iv) Climate- 
     resilient infrastructure; and (v) Livelihood diversification and 
     sustainable livelihoods. The objectives of this component will be achieved through the following activities: 
     (a) Soil and water conservation measures on community and privately cultivated lands, biological and physical 
     restoration of degraded conditions, check dams, near-dams, reservoirs, etc., to further reduce erosion, enhancing 
     productivity and profitability; (b) Implementation of improved land management practices, including the use of modern 
     technologies and techniques; (c) Creation of a community-based monitoring system for the evaluation of the impacts of the 
     proposed measures on the environment and livelihoods; (d) Development of a long-term plan to ensure the sustainability of the 
     activities; and (e) Training and capacity building for local communities, farmers, and other stakeholders. The following activities will be 
     funded by IDA and MDTF only in watersheds identified as having high potential for climate change adaptation and 
     mitigation.

<doc> By focusing on the most degraded watersheds in the Ethiopian highlands, the RLLP will target the communities most 
 vulnerable to climate change impacts. Under Component 1, the capacity at the local government level will increase 
 sensitivity of community to impacts affecting the environment and livelihoods. Information modernization activities will increase adaptive 
 3. activities to secure land tenure for small-holder farmers and improve access to high-quality 
 disaster risk reduction measures across highlands. The component will increase adaptive capacity at the regional 
 monitoring and ensuring a coordinated and consistent approach 
 regulations, models and research to enhance sustainable land use. 
 The project will work with the most vulnerable populations as target areas. Local-level 
 kebele level will ensure that interventions benefit smallholder 
 farmers. Under Component 2, the capacity at the local government level will increase household resources and encourage the 
 adoption of climate-smart agricultural practices in the rural sector. Under Component 
 3, the capacity at the local government level will increase adaptation to climate change and enhance disaster risk reduction 
 actions, in addition to capacity of NRLAIS under national and national level by introducing evidence-based 
 research into the development of policies, legislation, 
 and governance.

# Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project

## No-objection letter issued by the national designated authority(ies) or local point(s)

---

**To:** The Green Climate Fund (GCF)

**From:** Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change

**Date:** [Insert Date]

## 1. Introduction

This letter is issued to inform the Green Climate Fund (GCF) that the Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project has been approved by the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change in Ethiopia. The project aims to enhance the resilience of landscapes and livelihoods in the country to climate change impacts.

## 2. Background

The Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project is a key component of the national climate change adaptation strategy. It focuses on improving the adaptive capacity of ecosystems, communities, and institutions to cope with the effects of climate change. The project will address various challenges, including drought, flooding, and desertification, which are expected to exacerbate the impacts of climate change in Ethiopia.

## 3. Project Objectives

The primary objectives of the Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project are:

1. To enhance the adaptive capacity of ecosystems and communities to cope with the effects of climate change.
2. To improve the resilience of livelihoods in vulnerable areas, including agriculture, forestry, and fisheries.
3. To promote sustainable land use practices and reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and other land-use changes.
4. To strengthen institutional capacity to manage climate change risks and opportunities.

## 4. Project Implementation

The Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project will be implemented in the following phases:

1. Phase 1: Needs Assessment and Planning (2018-2020)
2. Phase 2: Project Design and Implementation (2021-2025)
3. Phase 3: Monitoring, Evaluation, and Learning (2026-2030)

## 5. Funding

The Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project will be funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) under the Global Environment Facility (GEF). The total project cost is estimated to be USD 10 million over five years.

## 6. Conclusion

We are pleased to announce that the Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project has been approved by the Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change in Ethiopia. We believe that this project will contribute significantly to enhancing the resilience of landscapes and livelihoods in the country to climate change impacts.

## 7. Contact Information

For any questions or concerns regarding the Resilient Landscapes and Livelihood Project, please contact:

Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change
[Insert Address]
[Insert Phone Number]
[Insert Email Address]
